Do not use LLM summarization or heuristic full-doc\n+digestion for the first agent index.\n@@\n - The manifest declares the runtime adapter, entrypoint file, and callable or\n   command shape.\n - Runtime execution receives argv/env according to the documented adapter\n   contract.\n-- Validation may import or load enough to verify the entrypoint, but normal\n-  execution should happen through the runner path.\n+- Validation checks runtime paths statically. Any import or execution-based smoke\n+  test is a separate trusted action, not the default validation path.\n \n Reasoning: external packs should not introduce a second execution model. The\n runner path is where output conventions, errors, and future sandbox wrapping\n belong.\n@@\n | Area | Right v1 level | Avoid in v1 |\n | --- | --- | --- |\n-| `PackResolver` | A concrete resource locator that resolves pack roots, component roots, docs, schemas, runtime files, examples, assets, and installed revisions. It should be the shared path used by validation, discovery, inspect, runtime, and agent-index. | A general plugin framework, dependency solver, trust engine, registry client, or runtime supervisor. |\n+| `PackResolver` | A read-only concrete resource locator that resolves pack roots, component roots, docs, schemas, runtime files, examples, and assets from known pack roots. It should be the shared path used by validation, discovery, inspect, runtime, and agent-index. | A general plugin framework, dependency solver, trust engine, registry client, install-state manager, or runtime supervisor. |\n+| `InstalledPackStore` | A small lifecycle/state boundary that owns install roots, staging directories, active pointers, per-revision install records, locks, update, uninstall, and rollback metadata. | A package manager, central database, dependency installer, marketplace client, or security policy engine. |\n | Trust tiers | A small enum or string field used for display and warnings: built-in, local, Git, future curated. | A full trust-policy language, permission graph, signing system, or sandbox substitute. |\n | Generated component inventory | A generated snapshot for install records, inspect output, summaries, and tests. Source of truth remains `pack.yaml` plus component manifests. | A manually authored `provides` file or a second registry format builders must maintain. |\n-| Install records | One per-pack JSON file under `.astrid/install.json`, written atomically and treated as authoritative for that installed pack. | A central database, multi-table revision store, or global package lock unless performance or rollback needs force it later. |\n+| Install records | One JSON file per installed revision under `.astrid/install.json`, written before activation and treated as authoritative for that revision. | A central database or global package lock unless performance needs force it later. |\n | Schemas and Python types | JSON Schema is the external contract. Python dataclasses or helpers may wrap already-validated data for internal ergonomics. | A parallel Pydantic contract hierarchy that can drift from published schemas. |\n | Agent index | Deterministic assembly from structured manifest fields, component metadata, docs paths, and concise stage summaries. | LLM-generated summaries, heuristic doc digestion, or prompt-injection machinery before structured fields prove insufficient. |\n*** End Patch\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Yes. I updated [docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md) to make that explicit.

The plan now has a final **Milestone 7: Pack Portfolio Rationalized** and a new **Sprint 9: Pack Portfolio Classification And Migration**.

The categories are:

- `core`: minimum platform packs that are always available
- `bundled installable`: first-party packs that ship with Astrid but use the installable contract
- `optional installable`: packs installed only when needed
- `local-only scratch`: project/user scratch, not public product surface
- `deprecated`: kept with warnings/aliases/migration notes
- `removed`: obsolete, no supported path forward

The sequencing is now: prove the migration on one real built-in pack first, then classify and migrate the full portfolio. That avoids trying to reorganize everything before the new contract has been proven.

> AGENT

Yes. We should not solve this with a pile of near-duplicate profiles.

What exists today:

- `--vendor claude|codex` already exists, but it only switches **premium slots** between Claude and Codex.
- It deliberately does **not** rewrite Hermes specs like DeepSeek/Kimi.
- So it cannot turn `hermes:fireworks:...deepseek...` into `hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro`.

Cleaner setup for this chain:

- Use the normal rubric profiles: `premium`, `thoughtful`, `basic`, etc.
- In the chain spec, define one reusable direct-DeepSeek override list:
  - `prep=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro`
  - `gate=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro`
  - `finalize=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro`
  - `execute=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro`
  - `loop_execute=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro`
- Apply that override list to milestones that otherwise use profiles with DeepSeek mechanical phases.

That keeps the intent visible: “use the normal profile, but route DeepSeek phases through the direct DeepSeek API.”

I removed the extra profile aliases I had started adding. The branch `megaplan/git-backed-packs-chain-setup` already exists locally and on GitHub, but I have not launched any chain or committed anything.

> DEVELOPER

whats' the right abstracton here?

> AGENT

The right abstraction is **model-provider routing**, not new profiles per project.

Profiles should answer: **which role uses which kind of model?**
Example: `thoughtful` means Claude plans/revises/reviews, DeepSeek handles mechanical phases.

Provider routing should answer: **when a profile says DeepSeek, which provider endpoint should satisfy it?**
Example: DeepSeek via direct API vs Fireworks.

So the clean model is:

```yaml
model_routing:
  deepseek-v4-pro: hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
  kimi-k2p6: hermes:fireworks:accounts/fireworks/models/kimi-k2p6
```

Then the chain can say:

```yaml
profile: thoughtful
deepseek_provider: direct
```

or, more generally:

```yaml
provider_overrides:
  deepseek: direct
```

That is better than:

- duplicating `thoughtful-deepseek-direct`
- duplicating `basic-deepseek-direct`
- hand-writing phase overrides on every milestone
- using `--vendor`, because `--vendor` is really Claude-vs-Codex, not provider routing

For this setup, since Megaplan does not appear to have a first-class `--deepseek-provider direct` flag today, the best practical abstraction is:

1. Keep normal profiles in the chain: `premium`, `thoughtful`, `basic`.
2. Define one YAML anchor in the chain spec for direct DeepSeek phase overrides.
3. Apply that anchor only where needed.

Example:

```yaml
x_direct_deepseek_phases: &direct_deepseek_phases
  - prep=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
  - gate=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
  - finalize=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
  - execute=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
  - loop_execute=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro

milestones:
  - label: sprint-2-resolver-runtime
    profile: thoughtful
    robustness: standard
    phase_model: *direct_deepseek_phases
```

Longer term, I’d add this to Megaplan itself:

```bash
megaplan init ... --provider deepseek=direct
```

or:

```bash
megaplan init ... --model-route deepseek-v4-pro=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
```

But for Astrid right now, YAML anchor + normal profiles is the right level: explicit, local to the chain, no profile explosion.

> DEVELOPER

How can we manage these multple cases?

> AGENT

Use a three-layer model:

1. **Role Profile**
What job each model is doing.

Examples:
- `thoughtful`: premium plans/reviews, DeepSeek executes/mechanical phases
- `premium`: premium model everywhere
- `basic`: DeepSeek-heavy, Kimi critique/review

2. **Provider Routing**
Where a model family is served from.

Examples:
- `deepseek = direct`
- `deepseek = fireworks`
- `kimi = fireworks`
- `kimi = moonshot/openrouter`

3. **Per-Run Overrides**
Exceptions for a specific sprint.

Examples:
- “Sprint 7 needs prep”
- “Use Codex instead of Claude”
- “Review with Claude even if execute is DeepSeek”

So in practice:

```yaml
defaults:
  profile: thoughtful
  provider_routes:
    deepseek: direct
    kimi: fireworks
  vendor: claude
  depth: medium

milestones:
  - label: sprint-1-contract-validation
    profile: premium
    depth: medium

  - label: sprint-5-builder-templates
    profile: basic
    robustness: light

  - label: sprint-7-elements
    profile: thoughtful
    with_prep: true
```

Megaplan today does not have clean first-class `provider_routes`, so represent it with YAML anchors in the chain spec:

```yaml
x_routes:
  direct_deepseek: &direct_deepseek
    - prep=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
    - gate=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
    - finalize=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
    - execute=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
    - loop_execute=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro

milestones:
  - label: sprint-2-runtime
    profile: thoughtful
    phase_model: *direct_deepseek
```

For maintainability, I’d standardize names:

- **Profiles:** `basic`, `thoughtful`, `premium`
- **Vendors:** `claude`, `codex`
- **Routes:** `deepseek_direct`, `deepseek_fireworks`, `kimi_fireworks`
- **Overrides:** only when a sprint has a real exception

Rule of thumb: if you need the same override in 3+ milestones, make it a named route. If it changes which model does which role, make it a profile. If it changes only where the model is hosted, make it provider routing.

id collision policy.\n+- Verify validation/static-smoke-test boundary.\n+- Verify dependency policy remains declaration-only.\n+- Update `docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md` if any decision docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/ideas/sprint-01-contract-validation.md\n+# Sprint 1: Pack Contract And Validation\n+\n+Use `docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md` as the source of truth.\n+\n+Goal: establish the external pack contract before changing runtime behavior.\n+\n+Scope:\n+\n+- Add JSON Schema contracts for `pack.yaml`, `executor.yaml`,\n+  `orchestrator.yaml`, and `element.yaml`.\n+- Add `schema_version` support and reject unknown schema versions.\n+- Parse author-facing YAML with PyYAML `safe_load`; do not use the flat parser\n+  for user-authored pack manifests.\n+- Implement `python3 -m astrid packs validate <path>` with static validation.\n+- Implement minimal `packs new <id>`, `executors new <pack>.<slug>`, and\n+  `orchestrators new <pack>.<slug>` scaffolds that validate.\n+- Add `examples/packs/minimal/` with one executor and one orchestrator.\n+- Add first-pass `docs/creating-packs.md`.\n+- Add focused tests for valid/invalid examples and scaffolds.\n+\n+Out of scope:\n+\n+- Git install.\n+- Installed-pack discovery.\n+- Runtime execution from installed packs.\n+- Element scaffolding.\n+- Dependency provisioning.\n+\n+Success criteria:\n+\n+- A builder can create a minimal pack and validate it.\n+- Broken pack examples fail with clear file-specific messages.\n+- Validation does not import or execute arbitrary pack code by default.\n*** Add File: docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/ideas/sprint-02-resolver-runtime.md\n+# Sprint 2: PackResolver, Deterministic Discovery, And Runtime Path\n+\n+Use `docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md` as the source of truth.\n+\n+Goal: replace accidental recursive discovery and split runtime resolution with a\n+single manifest-based path.\n+\n+Scope:\n+\n+- Introduce read-only `PackResolver` for pack roots, component roots, docs,\n+  schemas, runtime files, examples, and assets.\n+- Teach discovery to read declared content roots from `pack.yaml`.\n+- Preserve existing built-in list/search/inspect behavior while moving toward\n+  declared roots.\n+- Warn on likely pack directories with missing manifests and support explicit\n+  opt-outs such as `.no-pack`.\n+- Detect duplicate pack/component ids.\n+- Collapse orchestrator resolution to the manifest-based path.\n+- Resolve the legacy `builtin/hype.py` vs `builtin/hype/` split.\n+- Prove one external-style executor/orchestrator fixture runs through the\n+  standard CLI without being installed yet.\n+- Prove Sprint 1 scaffolds use the canonical runtime path.\n+\n+Out of scope:\n+\n+- Git install.\n+- Full installed-pack lifecycle.\n+- Rich component templates.\n+\n+Success criteria:\n+\n+- There is one canonical runtime resolution path.\n+- Stray manifests are not accidentally exposed.\n+- Built-in user-facing lists do not regress.\n*** Add File: docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/ideas/sprint-03-local-install.md\n+# Sprint 3: Local Pack Install, Trust UX, And Runnable Packs\n+\n+Use `docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md` as the source of truth.\n+\n+Goal: install a local external pack as a snapshot and make it runnable.\n+\n+Scope:\n+\n+- Add `InstalledPackStore` for install roots, staging directories, active\n+  pointers, per-revision install records, locks, update, uninstall, and rollback\n+  metadata.\n+- Implement `packs install <local-path>` and `packs install --dry-run\n+  <local-path>`.\n+- Respect the source repository's `.gitignore` for local copies.\n+- Validate before activation and leave no active pack on validation failure.\n+- Write per-revision `.astrid/install.json`.\n+- Implement `packs list`, `packs inspect`, `packs inspect --agent`,\n+  `packs update`, and `packs uninstall` for local installs.\n+- Include installed packs in list/search/inspect/run paths.\n+- Add validate -> install -> inspect -> run -> uninstall tests.\n+\n+Out of scope:\n+\n+- Git URL install.\n+- Automatic dependency provisioning.\n+- Marketplace/registry.\n+\n+Success criteria:\n+\n+- A local external pack can be installed, inspected, run, updated, and\n+  uninstalled.\n+- Install summaries show tools, entrypoints, declared secrets, dependencies,\n+  docs, source, and warnings.\n*** Add File: docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/ideas/sprint-04-git-install.md\n+# Sprint 4: Git Install, Pinning, And Update Safety\n+\n+Use `docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md` as the source of truth.\n+\n+Goal: support Git-backed packs without silently executing arbitrary branch tips.\n+\n+Scope:\n+\n+- Implement `packs install <git-url-or-path>` for Git URLs.\n+- Resolve and record a concrete commit SHA for every Git install.\n+- Show trust summary before activation unless `--yes` is passed.\n+- Revalidate before activation.\n+- Implement `packs update --dry-run` with manifest/component diff summary.\n+- Keep the previous active revision and support rollback.\n+- Use the user's existing Git credentials through the Git subprocess; do not\n+  manage GitHub tokens in v1.\n+\n+Out of scope:\n+\n+- Marketplace/curated registry.\n+- Signature verification.\n+- Dependency provisioning.\n+\n+Success criteria:\n+\n+- Git installs are pinned, inspectable, reversible, and validated before\n+  activation.\n+- Updates cannot silently swap executable code.\n*** Add File: docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/ideas/sprint-05-builder-scaffolding.md\n+# Sprint 5: Builder Scaffolding And Templates\n+\n+Use `docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md` as the source of truth.\n+\n+Goal: make pack authoring easy and hard to get subtly wrong beyond the minimal\n+Sprint 1 scaffolds.\n+\n+Scope:\n+\n+- Expand pack, executor, orchestrator, and element templates.\n+- Add `elements new`.\n+- Enrich `executors new` and `orchestrators new` with better docs, tests,\n+  examples, and option handling.\n+- Extract a small shared plan-v2 builder for orchestrator templates if the\n+  existing copy-paste remains.\n+- Update authoring docs to point at scaffolded examples instead of production\n+  internals.\n+\n+Out of scope:\n+\n+- Rich UI for authoring.\n+- Marketplace.\n+- Complex orchestrator generation beyond a minimal valid starting point.\n+\n+Success criteria:\n+\n+- Generated templates validate.\n+- Template-generated orchestrators use the canonical runtime path.\n+- Builders do not need to copy historical built-in packs to get started.\n*** Add File: docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/ideas/sprint-06-agent-index.md\n+# Sprint 6: Agent Index And Pack Legibility\n+\n+Use `docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md` as the source of truth.\n+\n+Goal: make installed packs easy for agents and humans to understand.\n+\n+Scope:\n+\n+- Implement `packs agent-index`.\n+- Improve `inspect --json` output for packs and pack components.\n+- Add guidance for writing pack-level `AGENTS.md`.\n+- Generate deterministic summaries from structured manifest fields, component\n+  metadata, docs paths, and bounded stage summaries.\n+- Keep Markdown supplemental; do not use LLM summarization or heuristic full-doc\n+  digestion in v1.\n+- Document secrets, dependencies, and when to use orchestrators vs executors.\n+\n+Out of scope:\n+\n+- Automatic prompt injection into every harness.\n+- Dependency installation.\n+- Hosted registry.\n+\n+Success constraints, and examples.\n*** Add File: docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/ideas/sprint-07-elements-example.md\n+# Sprint 7: Rich Example Pack And External Element Hardening\n+\n+Use example pack with at least one executor, orchestrator,\n+  element, schema, and example input.\n+- Run validate -> install -> inspect -> agent-index -> run on the example pack.\n+- Validate external element manifests.\n+- Include installed external elements in registry generation and typegen.\n+- Prove a rendered timeline can use an installed external element.\n+- Add regression tests and clear failure/debug paths for validation, registry,\n+  typegen, and render.\n+- Tighten validation around secrets, dependency declarations, and docs.\n+\n+Out of scope:\n+\n+- Full migration of every built-in pack.\n+- Marketplace.\n+- Sandbox execution.\n+\n+Success criteria:\n+\n+- The example resembles real user content, not only a minimal fixture.\n+- External elements work through render, not just validate/list/inspect.\n*** Add File: docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/ideas/sprint-08-legacy-migration-proof.md\n+# Sprint 8: Legacy Migration Proof\n+\n+Use `docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md` as the built-ins migrate immediately or through an\n+  alias/compatibility window.\n+- Update tests so the converted built-in pack uses the same resolver,\n+  validation, inspect, and runtime code as external packs.\n+\n+Out of scope:\n+\n+- Renaming every pack without aliases.\n+- Migrating every existing pack.\n+- Marketplace.\n+\n+Success criteria:\n+\n+- At least one real built-in proves the   "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/cloud.yaml\n+# Megaplan Cloud runner for the Git-backed packs chain.\n+#\n+# This spec is intentionally idle at boot. Launch the chain with\n+# `megaplan cloud chain ...` so cloud stages the local chain.yaml and idea files\n+# into the remote workspace before starting the persistent tmux run.\n+\n+provider: railway\n+\n+repo:\n+  url: https://github.com/peteromallet/Astrid.git\n+  branch: megaplan/git-backed-packs-chain-setup\n+  workspace: /workspace/astrid\n+\n+agents:\n+  default: codex\n+\n+codex:\n+  model: gpt-5.4\n+  reasoning: medium\n+\n+mode: idle\n+\n+megaplan:\n+  ref: main\n+\n+resources:\n+  volume: astrid-git-backed-packs-megaplan\n+  port: 8080\n+\n+railway:\n+  service: astrid-git-backed-packs\n+  session: astrid-git-backed-packs\n+\n+toolchains:\n+  - name: astrid-video-tools\n+    install: |\n+      RUN apt-get update \\\n+        && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends ffmpeg \\\n+        && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*\n+\n+secrets:\n+  - GITHUB_TOKEN\n+  - ANTHROPIC_API_KEY\n+  - DEEPSEEK_API_KEY\n+  - OPENAI_API_KEY\n*** Update File: docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/README.md\n@@\n-This directory prepares, but does not launch, the chained megaplan run for\n-`docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md`.\n+This directory prepares the chained megaplan run for\n+`docs/git-backed-packs-plan.md`.\n \n Run from the repository root only after the chain setup branch has been reviewed:\n \n ```bash\n megaplan chain start --spec docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/chain.yaml\n ```\n+\n+## Megaplan Cloud\n+\n+The cloud spec is at `docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/cloud.yaml`. It deploys a\n+Railway-backed runner from the isolated setup branch:\n+`megaplan/git-backed-packs-chain-setup`.\n+\n+Export the secrets named in the cloud spec before deploying:\n+\n+```bash\n+export GITHUB_TOKEN=...\n+export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...\n+export DEEPSEEK_API_KEY=...\n+export OPENAI_API_KEY=...\n+```\n+\n+Build and deploy the cloud runner:\n+\n+```bash\n+megaplan cloud build --cloud-yaml docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/cloud.yaml\n+megaplan cloud deploy --cloud-yaml docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/cloud.yaml\n+```\n+\n+Start the remote chain:\n+\n+```bash\n+megaplan cloud chain docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/chain.yaml \\\n+  --idea-dir docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/ideas \\\n+  --cloud-yaml docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/cloud.yaml\n+```\n+\n+Monitor it:\n+\n+```bash\n+megaplan cloud status --chain --cloud-yaml docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/cloud.yaml\n+megaplan cloud logs --cloud-yaml docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/cloud.yaml\n+megaplan cloud attach --cloud-yaml docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/cloud.yaml\n+```\n+\n+The runner boots in `idle` mode on purpose. `megaplan cloud chain` is the\n+preferred launch path because it uploads the chain spec and milestone idea\n+files to the remote workspace before starting `megaplan chain start` in the\n+`megaplan-chain` tmux session. The right recovery is remote-only: stop the failed chain session, delete the wrong first milestone branch it created from `main`, reset the remote workspace’s local `main` to the setup branch, clear the chain state, and restart `megaplan chain start --no-git-refresh`. That makes milestone branches base from the setup branch without merging anything into real `main`.

> DEVELOPER

how would you recomend we improve megaplan cloud based on this?

> AGENT

I’d make these Megaplan Cloud improvements, in this order:

1. **Add `repo.base_branch` / `chain.base_branch` support**
   The biggest issue was that `megaplan chain start` assumes `main`. For branch-isolated chains, Cloud should start from the configured `repo.branch` by default, or let the chain spec declare:
   ```yaml
   base_branch: megaplan/git-backed-packs-chain-setup
   ```
   Then milestone branches should be created from that, PRs should target that, and `main` should not be touched.

2. **Fix Railway scoping compatibility**
   Current Cloud passes `--project` to Railway commands that this CLI version does not accept. Cloud should either:
   - use `railway link` once in the generated deploy dir, then call unscoped commands, or
   - know which Railway subcommands support `--project`.
   
   This was the most annoying operational friction.

3. **Validate secrets before deploy with source hints**
   Cloud correctly failed on missing secrets, but it should print a structured preflight:
   ```text
   missing: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, DEEPSEEK_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY
   checked: environment only
   hint: source your .env or pass --env-file
   ```
   Even better: support:
   ```bash
   megaplan cloud deploy --env-file this.env --env-file ~/.hermes/.env
   ```

4. **Make `cloud chain --idea-dir` less footgunny**
   The current behavior duplicated paths when I passed the ideas directory. Cloud should detect whether milestone `idea:` paths are repo-relative and either:
   - resolve relative to repo root automatically, or
   - error with a precise message before trying upload.

5. **Add a true preflight command**
   Something like:
   ```bash
   megaplan cloud preflight --cloud-yaml ... --chain ...
   ```
   It should check:
   - Railway project/service exists or can be created
   - required secrets present
   - repo branch exists and remote HEAD matches local
   - chain idea files resolve
   - chain base branch is valid
   - `gh`/GitHub token can push and create PRs
   - model providers are reachable enough to avoid immediate auth failure

6. **Cloud chain should print the launch state**
   After `started megaplan-chain session`, it should automatically run status and tail the first useful log lines. The user should immediately see:
   ```text
   current milestone: sprint-00...
   plan: ...
   PR: #4
   status: in_progress
   ```

7. **Don’t make the user manually recover failed starts**
   If chain init fails before any execution, Cloud could offer:
   ```bash
   megaplan cloud chain restart --clean-failed-start
   ```
   That would kill the tmux session, clear chain state, delete the just-created empty branch if safe, and restart from the intended base.

8. **Expose Megaplan and repo provenance in status**
   `cloud status --chain` should include:
   ```text
   megaplan_ref: main
   megaplan_installed_sha: ...
   repo_branch: ...
   repo_head: ...
   repo_origin_branch_head: ...
   milestone_branch: ...
   milestone_base: ...
   ```
   That would answer “latest Megaplan/latest Astrid?” without remote exec spelunking.

9. **Create Railway service if missing**
   Since the spec declares `railway.service`, deploy should create it if absent or give a one-command fix. I had to do `railway add --service ...` manually.

10. **Document the branch-isolated chain pattern**
   The docs should explicitly show the “setup branch -> milestone branches -> PRs back to setup branch -> later merge setup branch to main” pattern. That is exactly what we wanted here, and Cloud should make it first-class.

> DEVELOPER

$megaplan-rubric

> AGENT

Using `$megaplan-rubric`, I’d scope the Megaplan Cloud improvements like this:

**Sprint A: Cloud Correctness Fixes**
Profile: `thoughtful/standard/medium`

Covers:
- `repo.branch` / chain base branch semantics.
- `cloud chain` using the right base branch instead of hardcoded `main`.
- PR target branch matching the chain base.
- Safer restart/recovery for failed chain starts.
- Provenance in `cloud status --chain`.

Why: this is cross-cutting and correctness-sensitive, but the decisions are now mostly known from the failure. `thoughtful` is enough; `premium` feels unnecessary unless the branch semantics touch many more internals than expected.

**Sprint B: Railway Provider Hardening**
Profile: `led/light` or `thoughtful/light`

Covers:
- Railway CLI scoping compatibility.
- Create service if missing.
- Link generated deploy dirs predictably.
- Better Railway preflight.

I’d start this as `led/light` if the Railway provider code is isolated. Escalate to `thoughtful/light` if the provider abstraction is messy.

**Sprint C: UX / Preflight / Docs**
Profile: `basic/light`

Covers:
- `cloud preflight`.
- Better `--idea-dir` validation.
- Secret missing hints.
- Status after launch.
- Docs for branch-isolated cloud chains.

This is mostly glue, validation, and docs once Sprint A settles the semantics.

If we only do one sprint right now, I’d pick **`thoughtful/standard/medium`** and include the highest-leverage fixes: base branch support, Railway scoping fix, secret/env-file support, and `cloud chain` preflight. That’s a coherent 2-week sprint and directly addresses every failure we hit.

> DEVELOPER

open the sill doc what's best?

> DEVELOPER

what's basic*

> AGENT

`basic` is Megaplan’s Tier 1 profile.

It means: use cheap/open models for the whole run.

Typical phase shape:

```text
plan/finalize/execute: DeepSeek
critique/review: Kimi
```

Best for:

- routine programming
- small features in known patterns
- config changes
- utility scripts
- mechanical refactors
- docs/discovery
- schema migrations where the shape is obvious
- glue code with few overlapping concerns

Not best for:

- new architecture
- high-stakes schema/contracts
- tricky cross-system behavior
- anything where the planning itself needs premium judgment

The `*` is not a special notation in the rubric. If you meant `basic/light`, that means Tier 1 plus one critique pass before execution. If you meant `basic/tiny`, that is even lighter: plan → finalize → execute, no critique/review.

```yaml
profile: thoughtful
phase_model:
  - plan=codex:medium
  - critique=codex:low
  - revise=codex:medium
  - review=codex:low
  - prep=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
  - gate=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
  - finalize=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
  - execute=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
  - loop_execute=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro
```

That matches `thoughtful//medium @codex` in spirit: Codex owns the reasoning loop; DeepSeek handles mechanical phases.

I’d do that for the **3 cloud-improvement sprints**, not blindly for the 10-pack implementation chain unless you want me to re-scope that chain too.

> DEVELOPER

wait, why did that fail? Why did it even need Shannon? DOes this point to another gab?

> AGENT

It failed because Sprint 0 is configured as:

```yaml
profile: premium
```

In the current rubric/profile map, `premium` defaults to Claude for all phases. So the first phase was:

```text
megaplan plan -> claude:low
```

Current Megaplan’s Claude route apparently goes through **Shannon** as the adapter/wrapper, and the cloud image did not install Shannon. The image installed:

```text
@openai/codex
@anthropic-ai/claude-code
```

but not:

```text
@dexh/shannon@0.0.2
```

So this was not a planning failure. It was an agent-runtime dependency gap.

Yes, it points to a real Megaplan Cloud gap:

1. **Cloud image is not aligned with latest Megaplan agent dependencies**
   If Megaplan `main` requires Shannon for Claude routes, the Dockerfile should install it, or Megaplan should expose an install/doctor command Cloud runs at build time.

2. **Cloud preflight should catch this before launch**
   It should inspect the resolved chain profiles and say:
   ```text
   required agents: claude, deepseek
   missing runtime deps: shannon
   ```

3. **`agents.default: codex` in cloud.yaml does not override profile phases**
   That setting configures default routing, but the chain milestone’s `profile: premium` explicitly selected Claude phases. That distinction is too easy to misunderstand.

4. **Profiles are not self-describing enough at cloud launch**
   `cloud chain` should print the resolved phase map for each milestone before starting:
   ```text
   sprint-00: premium -> plan=claude:low, execute=claude:low...
   ```

5. **This strengthens the case for `thoughtful @codex`**
   If we switch the relevant sprints to Codex-owned reasoning phases, we avoid Shannon entirely for those phases. But if any Claude phase remains, Shannon still needs to be installed.

Best fix: do both. Install Shannon in the cloud image because Claude is a valid route, and update this chain to the intended `thoughtful//medium @codex`-style phase map where that is now the desired profile.

> DEVELOPER

but why are we even using Claude?

> AGENT

We were using Claude because the chain spec still had `profile: premium` for Sprints 0-1, and built-in `premium` resolves to Claude phases. Also, built-in `thoughtful` still uses Claude for plan/critique/revise/review unless `phase_model` overrides it. `agents.default: codex` in `cloud.yaml` does not override an explicit profile.

For this relaunch, we do not need Claude. I’m changing the chain to make Codex explicit for the reasoning phases and direct DeepSeek for mechanical phases. I’m keeping the Shannon/Bun install fix in the cloud config because it is a real Megaplan Cloud dependency gap for any future Claude route, but the relaunched Astrid chain should not depend on Claude/Shannon.

Durable cloud images need unzip, Bun, Shannon, and stable symlinks if Claude/Shannon routes are supported.\n- `cloud.yaml agents.default: codex` did not override explicit profile phase routing. Cloud should make resolved phase maps/dependencies visible before launch.\n- Railway CLI scoping had compatibility friction: this installed Railway CLI accepts `--project` on `railway link`, but not on `railway variables`/`ssh` in the way the provider currently emits.\n- Chain startup hardcoded `main` for git refresh, milestone branch creation, and PR base. Branch-isolated chains need a first-class base branch; milestone branches and PRs should target that base rather than production `main`.\n- `cloud chain --idea-dir docs/.../ideas` duplicated repo-relative idea paths; preflight should detect this and recommend repo root or resolve intelligently.\n\nDeliverables:\n1. Cloud image/install approach:\n   - Update the Megaplan Cloud Docker/template install path so every runtime dependency required by supported agents is installed and on PATH.\n   - Include Shannon, Bun, and unzip in the durable install path if Claude routes require Shannon.\n   - Prefer a maintainable install helper or explicit template block over ad hoc project-local cloud.yaml hacks.\n   - Add/adjust tests that render the Dockerfile/entrypoint and verify the required commands are installed/symlinked in the generated template text.\n\n2. Agent dependency preflight:\n   - Add a cloud preflight path, or extend `cloud chain`/`cloud deploy`, so it resolves the chain milestone phase maps before starting and reports required runtime deps.\n   - It should catch missing `shannon`, `bun`, `claude`, `codex`, `tmux`, and provider/env requirements before launching a remote chain.\n   - It should explain that `agents.default` does not override explicit profile/phase-model choices.\n\n3. Branch-isolated chain correctness:\n   - Add first-class base branch support for chain/cloud-chain runs. Prefer a chain-spec field such as `base_branch`, defaulting to cloud `repo.branch` when run via cloud, else `main` for backward compatibility.\n   - Use the base branch for git refresh, milestone branch creation, and PR base.\n   - Avoid requiring `--no-git-refresh` hacks to run a chain from a setup branch.\n   - Include tests for milestone branch creation and PR base behavior.\n\n4. Cloud chain path/launch UX:\n   - Make `cloud chain --idea-dir` robust for repo-relative idea paths; fail early with a precise message or resolve from repo root.\n   - After launching a chain, print status/provenance: current milestone, plan name, PR number, repo branch/head, chain base branch, Megaplan ref/install source, and resolved phase map summary.\n\n5. Railway provider compatibility:\n   - Make Railway project/service scoping compatible with current Railway CLI behavior.\n   - If a service named in cloud.yaml is absent, create it or emit one exact command to create/link it.\n   - Avoid passing unsupported `--project` flags to subcommands that reject them.\n\nConstraints:\n- Keep this as a coherent Sprint A implementation. Do not implement later UX polish that is not necessary to prevent the failures above.\n- Preserve existing provider behavior for local and ssh providers.\n- Add focused tests; do not rely on live Railway for unit coverage.\n- Use `thoughtful//medium @codex` for this run: Codex author/reasoning, direct DeepSeek/mechanical phases where the profile uses them.\nEOF\nmegaplan init --project-dir /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan --name cloud-runtime-correctness-20260514 --profile thoughtful --vendor codex --depth medium --robustness standard --auto-approve --idea-file /tmp/megaplan-cloud-sprints/sprint-a-cloud-runtime-correctness.md --auto-start",

> DEVELOPER

Does DEepseek PAI have any restrictons that could cause issues? Max tokens, etc.?

> AGENT

I’m going to check DeepSeek’s current official API docs before answering, because model limits and endpoint behavior are exactly the kind of thing that changes.

> AGENT

Yes. The main DeepSeek API constraints that could bite Megaplan are:

1. **Dynamic concurrency/rate limits**
   DeepSeek says limits are dynamic based on load and usage. If hit, requests return `429`. This can make parallel chain phases flaky unless Megaplan backs off/retries.

2. **Queue/start timeout**
   If a request has not started inference after **10 minutes**, DeepSeek closes the connection. That can look like a stale worker unless the harness treats keep-alives and closed connections correctly.

3. **Keep-alive response noise**
   While waiting, non-streaming calls may emit empty lines; streaming calls may emit `: keep-alive` comments. Parsers must ignore these. Otherwise the harness can misclassify a healthy queued request as malformed/stuck.

4. **Token limits depend on model/route**
   For current `deepseek-v4-pro`, official docs list **1M context** and **384K max output**, so token limit is probably not the main issue for our sprint plans.
   But older/legacy `deepseek-chat` / `deepseek-reasoner` docs list **64K context**, `deepseek-reasoner` **32K CoT**, and **8K final output**, which absolutely could truncate final plans/reviews if the harness accidentally routes to those aliases.

5. **Thinking tokens count**
   Reasoning/thinking output can consume output budget. If Megaplan asks for huge structured JSON plus long reasoning, `finish_reason="length"` can cut off the response and break JSON parsing.

6. **Transient service errors**
   DeepSeek documents `500` server errors and `503` overload. These need retry with backoff, not immediate plan failure.

So the practical answer: **yes, but the riskiest issues are dynamic concurrency, 10-minute queue close, keep-alive parsing, and accidental route/model mismatch**, not raw context size if we are truly using `deepseek-v4-pro` on the official DeepSeek API.

Sources: DeepSeek rate limits, error codes, model pricing/limits, reasoning model docs:
- https://api-docs.deepseek.com/quick_start/rate_limit/
- https://api-docs.deepseek.com/quick_start/error_codes
- https://api-docs.deepseek.com/quick_start/pricing/
- https://api-docs.deepseek.com/guides/reasoning_model

> DEVELOPER

i mean in our setup, do we have any constraints

> AGENT

I’ll inspect our Megaplan/Hermes DeepSeek routing and config, not the generic DeepSeek docs. I’m looking for explicit caps, timeout budgets, streaming handling, and whether we’re actually hitting `deepseek-v4-pro` or a legacy alias.

> AGENT

Yes, in our setup there are a few real constraints.

For the Astrid chain, DeepSeek is used only for `prep`, `gate`, `finalize`, `execute`, and `loop_execute`; `plan`, `critique`, `revise`, `review` are Codex. That’s defined in [chain.yaml](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/chain.yaml). It is using direct DeepSeek API via `hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro`, not Fireworks.

The main Megaplan-side limits are:

- **Output cap:** Hermes sets `max_tokens=32768` for non-execute phases and `65536` for execute phases in [hermes_worker.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/hermes_worker.py). This is below DeepSeek V4’s advertised max, but still a practical cap. Very large `finalize.json` / `execution.json` outputs can still truncate or fail JSON parsing.

- **Timeouts:** general worker timeout is `7200s`; auto driver phase timeout is `3600s`; idle timeout is `900s`. So DeepSeek’s own 10-minute queue close can happen before Megaplan gives up, and may show up as a failed/stale phase.

- **Streaming behavior:** our code only forces streaming for `fireworks:*` when `max_tokens > 4096`. It does **not** force streaming for `deepseek:*`. That is probably okay with the OpenAI-compatible client, but it means direct DeepSeek keep-alive/empty-line behavior depends on the lower client handling it cleanly.

- **429 handling:** direct DeepSeek 429s cool down the key for `120s`, but I do not see the same robust automatic provider fallback that MiniMax has. So a DeepSeek rate/concurrency issue can pause or fail that phase rather than seamlessly switching.

- **Reasoning mode:** our Hermes worker explicitly tries to disable reasoning for `deepseek-v4-*` so structured JSON lands in `content`, not reasoning fields. Good guardrail, but if the API ignores that field or behavior changes, JSON phases are vulnerable.

Current practical risk: **not token context**, more likely **DeepSeek queue/concurrency/timeout behavior plus JSON-output fragility on large execute/finalize phases**. For the local Megaplan Cloud hardening run, we routed execution through Codex, so DeepSeek constraints are mostly not in that current local execute path.

> AGENT

I’d **not** change Fireworks if it was working perfectly.

Recommendation:

- **Fireworks DeepSeek:** leave behavior as-is. Don’t force reasoning off. It already worked, and changing reasoning semantics risks breaking a stable route.
- **Direct DeepSeek API:** keep reasoning/thinking disabled for Megaplan structured phases unless we prove it returns valid JSON reliably with reasoning enabled.
- **Max tokens:** increase only for **direct DeepSeek**, not Fireworks. Fireworks has its own `max_tokens > 4096 requires stream=true` constraint, and our current code already handles that.

So the right move is narrower than what I was about to do:

1. Set higher max output for `deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro`.
2. Keep direct DeepSeek reasoning disabled for `gate/finalize/execute` because Megaplan needs strict JSON/content.
3. Do **not** touch Fireworks reasoning behavior.
4. Do **not** globally enable reasoning until we have a test showing the parser handles DeepSeek reasoning fields correctly.

Your instinct is right: if Fireworks was stable, we shouldn’t generalize a direct-API concern onto it.

tool_use apply_patch
```json
{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/hermes_worker.py\n@@\n-# Fireworks rejects requests with `max_tokens > 4096` unless `stream=true`.\n-# When that threshold applies we flip the call to streaming and let\n-# run_agent's streaming path reassemble the response into the same shape the\n-# rest of megaplan expects.  Streaming lives entirely inside the worker —\n-# downstream callers never see streaming semantics.\n-_FIREWORKS_STREAM_MAX_TOKENS = 4096\n+# Fireworks rejects requests with `max_tokens > 4096` unless `stream=true`.\n+# Direct DeepSeek accepts high-token non-streaming calls, but streaming keeps\n+# the transport closer to the Fireworks path and avoids quiet long-poll gaps.\n+# Streaming lives entirely inside the worker; downstream callers never see\n+# streaming semantics.\n+_HIGH_TOKEN_STREAM_MAX_TOKENS = 4096\n+_HIGH_TOKEN_STREAM_PROVIDERS = (\"fireworks:\", \"deepseek:\")\n@@\n def _provider_requires_streaming(model: str | None, max_tokens: int | None) -> bool:\n     \"\"\"Return True when this provider/max_tokens pair must use streaming.\n \n-    Today only Fireworks has this constraint (max_tokens > 4096 must stream).\n-    Other providers (OpenRouter, DeepSeek-direct, MiniMax, etc.) accept\n-    non-streamed high-max_tokens requests, so we keep the opt-in narrow.\n+    Fireworks requires streaming above the threshold. Direct DeepSeek is kept on\n+    the same high-token streaming path so `deepseek:*` behaves like the known\n+    good Fireworks DeepSeek route.\n     \"\"\"\n     if not model or not isinstance(model, str):\n         return False\n-    if not model.startswith(\"fireworks:\"):\n+    if not model.startswith(_HIGH_TOKEN_STREAM_PROVIDERS):\n         return False\n     if max_tokens is None:\n         return False\n-    return max_tokens > _FIREWORKS_STREAM_MAX_TOKENS\n+    return max_tokens > _HIGH_TOKEN_STREAM_MAX_TOKENS\n@@\n def _streaming_run_kwargs(model: str | None, max_tokens: int | None) -> dict:\n     \"\"\"Build the run_conversation kwargs needed to force streaming when required.\"\"\"\n     if _provider_requires_streaming(model, max_tokens):\n         return {\"stream_callback\": _no_op_stream}\n     return {}\n+\n+\n+def _reasoning_config_for_model(resolved_model: str | None) -> dict | None:\n+    \"\"\"Return a reasoning override only for models known to need one.\n+\n+    DeepSeek V4 worked through Fireworks without a reasoning override. Keep the\n+    direct DeepSeek API route aligned with that behavior: do not send\n+    `thinking: disabled` or an equivalent override for `deepseek-v4-*`.\n+    \"\"\"\n+    model_lower = (resolved_model or \"\").lower()\n+    reasoning_off_families = (\n+        \"qwen/qwen3\",\n+        \"deepseek/deepseek-r1\",\n+    )\n+    if any(model_lower.startswith(prefix) for prefix in reasoning_off_families):\n+        return {\"enabled\": False}\n+    return None\n@@\n-    # Disable reasoning/thinking for models that default to reasoning mode.\n-    # When reasoning is enabled, some models (Qwen3.5, DeepSeek-R1) put all\n-    # output in the reasoning field and return content: null.  Megaplan needs\n-    # structured JSON in the content field, so force reasoning off for any\n-    # model family known to use reasoning by default on OpenRouter.\n-    _model_lower = (resolved_model or \"\").lower()\n-    _reasoning_families = (\n-        \"qwen/qwen3\",\n-        \"deepseek/deepseek-r1\",\n-        \"deepseek-v4-\",\n-        \"deepseek/deepseek-v4-\",\n-    )\n-    _reasoning_off = (\n-        {\"enabled\": False}\n-        if any(_model_lower.startswith(prefix) for prefix in _reasoning_families)\n-        else None\n-    )\n+    # Disable reasoning/thinking only for model families that otherwise return\n+    # structured output outside the normal content field. DeepSeek V4 is not in\n+    # this list so the direct API route matches the Fireworks route.\n+    _reasoning_off = _reasoning_config_for_model(resolved_model)\n*** End Patch\n"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

Why not push to githuba na dpull there/

> AGENT

That is the better path. We should **not** keep editing cloud `site-packages` by hand.

Correct sequence:

1. Finish or pause the local Megaplan hardening execution cleanly.
2. Commit the Megaplan changes on `megaplan/cloud-runtime-correctness-sprint`.
3. Push that branch to GitHub.
4. On the Astrid cloud machine, install/pull Megaplan from that branch, or update `cloud.yaml` `megaplan.ref` to that branch and redeploy.
5. Resume/continue the Astrid chain.

Why this is better:

- It is reproducible.
- The cloud machine can be rebuilt without losing the patch.
- We avoid mixed-runtime weirdness from hand-patching installed Python files.
- The DeepSeek/Fireworks alignment travels with tests.
- The same branch includes the cloud hardening work that fixes the exact friction we hit.

The only caution: the local hardening execution is still mid-run. I’d normally let it finish review before pushing. But since you want this on cloud now, I can push the current branch as an in-progress branch and point the cloud machine at it, then continue monitoring.

and\n-  (b) when Fireworks does return a real 400 (or any other error), propagate\n+  (a) detect [REDACTED] models and force the streaming\n+      path, with reassembly hidden from the rest of megaplan; and\n+  (b) when a provider returns a real 400 (or any other error), propagate\n       it as a hard failure rather than silently degrading to empty output.\n@@\n-def test_provider_requires_streaming_only_fires_for_fireworks_with_high_max_tokens() -> None:\n+def test_provider_requires_streaming_for_high_token_fireworks_and_direct_deepseek() -> None:\n@@\n-    # Other providers don't need this even at very high max_tokens\n+    # Direct DeepSeek mirrors the Fireworks high-token streaming path.\n+    assert _provider_requires_streaming(\"deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro\", 16384) is True\n+    assert _provider_requires_streaming(\"deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro\", 4096) is False\n+\n+    # Other providers don't need this even at very high max_tokens\n     assert _provider_requires_streaming(\"openrouter/anthropic/claude\", 16384) is False\n-    assert _provider_requires_streaming(\"deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro\", 16384) is False\n     assert _provider_requires_streaming(\"minimax:MiniMax-M2\", 16384) is False\n     assert _provider_requires_streaming(None, 16384) is False\n@@\n-    # Other provider → empty kwargs (no streaming forced)\n+    # Direct DeepSeek high max_tokens mirrors Fireworks.\n+    assert \"stream_callback\" in _streaming_run_kwargs(\"deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro\", 8192)\n+\n+    # Other provider → empty kwargs (no streaming forced)\n     assert _streaming_run_kwargs(\"openrouter/anthropic/claude\", 16384) == {}\n     assert _streaming_run_kwargs(\"fireworks:foo\", 4096) == {}\n*** End Patch\n"
}

> AGENT

Set up.

Current behavior on cloud:

- Single PR branch: `megaplan/git-backed-packs/sprint-02-resolver-runtime`
- Single PR: `#8`
- Runner session: `megaplan-chain-one-pr`
- It runs one milestone at a time with `--no-push --no-git-refresh --one`
- After each completed sprint, it does:
  - `git add -A`
  - commit: `megaplan: remaining-chain-sprint-N`
  - push to the same branch/PR #8
- Then it continues to the next sprint

Current live state:

- Sprint 2 plan: `sprint-2-packresolver-20260514-2152`
- Active step: `critique via codex`
- Health: healthy
- Branch is clean against origin right now, because Sprint 2 has not completed and pushed yet.

{
  "cmd": "railway ssh --service astrid-git-backed-packs -- 'cat > /workspace/astrid/.megaplan/run-one-pr-push-each-sprint.sh <<'\\''SH'\\''\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\nset -u\ncd /workspace/astrid\nLOG=/workspace/astrid/.megaplan/cloud-chain-one-pr-push-each.log\nSPEC=/workspace/astrid/docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/chain.yaml\nBRANCH=megaplan/git-backed-packs/sprint-02-resolver-runtime\ncommit_and_push() {\n  local label=\"$1\"\n  git status --short --branch\n  git add -A\n  if git diff --cached --quiet; then\n    echo \"[$label] no changes to commit\"\n  else\n    git commit -m \"megaplan: ${label}\"\n    git push origin HEAD:\"$BRANCH\"\n  fi\n}\n{\n  echo \"=== one-pr push-each-sprint start $(date -Is) ===\"\n  git checkout \"$BRANCH\"\n  git status --short --branch\n  gh pr edit 8 --title \"Git-backed packs remaining chain\" --body \"Runs the remaining git-backed packs milestones on a single branch/PR. Sprint 0 and Sprint 1 are already merged; this PR accumulates Sprint 2 onward and is pushed after each sprint.\" || true\n  for n in $(seq 1 8); do\n    echo \"=== loop $n status before $(date -Is) ===\"\n    status=\"$(megaplan chain status --spec \"$SPEC\" 2>&1)\"\n    echo \"$status\"\n    if ! echo \"$status\" | grep -Eq \"\\[in_progress\\]|\\[pending\\]\"; then\n      echo \"No pending/in-progress milestones remain\"\n      break\n    fi\n    echo \"=== loop $n run one milestone $(date -Is) ===\"\n    MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 megaplan chain start --spec \"$SPEC\" --no-git-refresh --no-push --one\n    rc=$?\n    echo \"=== loop $n chain rc=$rc $(date -Is) ===\"\n    megaplan chain status --spec \"$SPEC\" || true\n    if [ \"$rc\" -ne 0 ]; then\n      echo \"Chain failed in loop $n; leaving worktree for inspection\"\n      exit \"$rc\"\n    fi\n    current_label=\"$(megaplan chain status --spec \"$SPEC\" 2>/dev/null | awk '/\\[completed\\]/ {label=$3} END {gsub(/[()]/,\"\",label); print label}')\"\n    if [ -z \"$current_label\" ]; then current_label=\"chain milestone $n\"; fi\n    commit_and_push \"$current_label\"\n  done\n  echo \"=== one-pr push-each-sprint finished $(date -Is) ===\"\n  megaplan chain status --spec \"$SPEC\" || true\n  git status --short --branch\n} >> \"$LOG\" 2>&1\nSH\nchmod +x /workspace/astrid/.megaplan/run-one-pr-push-each-sprint.sh\ntmux new-session -d -s megaplan-chain-one-pr -c /workspace/astrid /workspace/astrid/.megaplan/run-one-pr-push-each-sprint.sh\nsleep 5\ntmux has-session -t megaplan-chain-one-pr && echo one-pr-loop-running\ntail -80 /workspace/astrid/.megaplan/cloud-chain-one-pr-push-each.log\n'",
  "yield_time_ms": 1000,
  "max_output_tokens": 18000,
  "workdir": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid"
}

_module_name_from_resolver_metadata(orchestrator: OrchestratorDefinition) -> str:\n+    root_raw = orchestrator.metadata.get(\"orchestrator_root\")\n+    if not isinstance(root_raw, str) or not root_raw:\n+        raise OrchestratorRunnerError(\n+            f\"orchestrator {orchestrator.id!r} has no resolver-backed component root\"\n+        )\n+    runtime_file = orchestrator.metadata.get(\"runtime_file\", \"run.py\")\n+    if not isinstance(runtime_file, str) or not runtime_file:\n+        raise OrchestratorRunnerError(f\"orchestrator {orchestrator.id!r} has no runtime file\")\n+    runtime_path = (Path(root_raw) / runtime_file).resolve()\n+    if not runtime_path.is_file():\n+        raise OrchestratorRunnerError(\n+            f\"runtime file not found for orchestrator {orchestrator.id!r}: {runtime_path}\"\n+        )\n+    try:\n+        from .runtime import resolve_python_module_from_file\n+\n+        module_name = resolve_python_module_from_file(runtime_path)\n+    except Exception:\n+        module_name = None\n+    if module_name:\n+        try:\n+            module = importlib.import_module(module_name)\n+        except Exception as exc:\n+            raise OrchestratorRunnerError(\n+                f\"failed to import orchestrator runtime module {module_name!r}: {exc}\"\n+            ) from exc\n+    else:\n+        module_name = _file_module_name(orchestrator, runtime_path)\n+        spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(module_name, runtime_path)\n+        if spec is None or spec.loader is None:\n+            raise OrchestratorRunnerError(\n+                f\"cannot load orchestrator runtime file for {orchestrator.id!r}: {runtime_path}\"\n+            )\n+        module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)\n+        sys.modules[module_name] = module\n+        try:\n+            spec.loader.exec_module(module)\n+        except Exception as exc:\n+            raise OrchestratorRunnerError(\n+                f\"failed to execute orchestrator runtime file {runtime_path}: {exc}\"\n+            ) from exc\n+    orchestrator.metadata[\"_loaded_python_module\"] = module  # type: ignore[index]\n+    return module_name\n+\n+\n+def _file_module_name(orchestrator: OrchestratorDefinition, runtime_path: Path) -> str:\n+    digest = abs(hash(str(runtime_path))) % (10**12)\n+    slug = re.sub(r\"[^A-Za-z0-9_]\", \"_\", orchestrator.id)\n+    return f\"_astrid_pack_runtime_{slug}_{digest}\"\n+\n+\n+def _invoke_python_orchestrator_target(\n+    target: Any,\n+    orchestrator: OrchestratorDefinition,\n+    request: OrchestratorRunRequest,\n+) -> Any:\n+    module = orchestrator.metadata.get(\"_loaded_python_module\")\n+    if orchestrator.runtime.module:\n+        return target(request, orchestrator)\n+    if _looks_like_cli_entrypoint(target):\n+        return target(_python_cli_argv(request))\n+    return target(\n+        inputs=dict(request.inputs),\n+        outputs=dict(request.outputs),\n+        out=Path(request.out).expanduser().resolve() if request.out is not None else None,\n+        brief=Path(request.brief).expanduser().resolve() if request.brief is not None else None,\n+        project=request.project,\n+        dry_run=request.dry_run,\n+        verbose=request.verbose,\n+        thread=request.thread,\n+        variants=request.variants,\n+        from_ref=request.from_ref,\n+        orchestrator_args=tuple(request.orchestrator_args),\n+        request=request,\n+        orchestrator=orchestrator,\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def _looks_like_cli_entrypoint(target: Any) -> bool:\n+    try:\n+        signature = inspect.signature(target)\n+    except (TypeError, ValueError):\n+        return False\n+    positional = [\n+        parameter\n+        for parameter in signature.parameters.values()\n+        if parameter.kind in (parameter.POSITIONAL_ONLY, parameter.POSITIONAL_OR_KEYWORD)\n+    ]\n+    keyword_only = [\n+        parameter\n+        for parameter in signature.parameters.values()\n+        if parameter.kind == parameter.KEYWORD_ONLY\n+        and parameter.default is parameter.empty\n+    ]\n+    return len(positional) <= 1 and not keyword_only\n+\n+\n+def _python_cli_argv(request: OrchestratorRunRequest) -> list[str]:\n+    argv: list[str] = []\n+    if request.out is not None:\n+        argv.extend([\"--out\", str(Path(request.out).expanduser().resolve())])\n+    if request.brief is not None:\n+        argv.extend([\"--brief\", str(Path(request.brief).expanduser().resolve())])\n+    for key, value in request.inputs.items():\n+        argv.extend([\"--input\", f\"{key}={_stringify_value(value)}\"])\n+    argv.extend(request.orchestrator_args)\n+    return argv\n*** End Patch\n"
}

signature.parameters.values()\n         if parameter.kind == parameter.KEYWORD_ONLY\n         and parameter.default is parameter.empty\n     ]\n+\n+\n+def _accepted_kwargs(target: Any, kwargs: Mapping[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n+    try:\n+        signature = inspect.signature(target)\n+    except (TypeError, ValueError):\n+        return dict(kwargs)\n+    if any(parameter.kind == parameter.VAR_KEYWORD for parameter in signature.parameters.values()):\n+        return dict(kwargs)\n+    accepted = {\n+        name\n+        for name, parameter in signature.parameters.items()\n+        if parameter.kind in (parameter.POSITIONAL_OR_KEYWORD, parameter.KEYWORD_ONLY)\n+    }\n+    return {key: value for key, value in kwargs.items() if key in accepted}\n*** End Patch\n"

{
  "cmd": "megaplan cloud exec --cloud-yaml docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/cloud.yaml 'cd /workspace/astrid && cat > .megaplan/supervise-one-pr-chain.sh <<'\\''EOF'\\''\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\nset -u\ncd /workspace/astrid\nLOG=/workspace/astrid/.megaplan/hourly-chain-supervisor.log\nSPEC=/workspace/astrid/docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/chain.yaml\nBRANCH=megaplan/git-backed-packs/sprint-02-resolver-runtime\nlog(){ echo \"[$(date -Is)] $*\"; }\ncurrent_plan(){ megaplan chain status --spec \"$SPEC\" 2>/dev/null | jq -r \".summary.current_plan_name // empty\" 2>/dev/null || true; }\nplan_state(){ local p=\"$1\"; [ -n \"$p\" ] && jq -r \".current_state // empty\" \"/workspace/astrid/.megaplan/plans/$p/state.json\" 2>/dev/null || true; }\nquota_failed(){ local p=\"$1\"; [ -n \"$p\" ] && jq -r \".latest_failure.message? // empty, .history[-1].message? // empty\" \"/workspace/astrid/.megaplan/plans/$p/state.json\" 2>/dev/null | grep -qi \"quota\"; }\nstart_runner(){\n  if tmux has-session -t megaplan-chain-one-pr 2>/dev/null; then log \"runner already active\"; return; fi\n  log \"starting one-PR chain runner\"\n  tmux new-session -d -s megaplan-chain-one-pr \"/workspace/astrid/.megaplan/run-one-pr-push-each-sprint.sh\"\n}\nrecover_quota_plan(){\n  local p=\"$1\" state_file=\"/workspace/astrid/.megaplan/plans/$p/state.json\"\n  [ -f \"$state_file\" ] || return 1\n  log \"recovering quota-failed plan $p with direct DeepSeek routing\"\n  cp \"$state_file\" \"$state_file.supervisor-before-$(date +%Y%m%d%H%M%S)\"\n  jq '\\'' .current_state=\"initialized\" | .config.phase_model = (.config.phase_model | map(if test(\"^(plan|critique|revise|review|loop_plan)=codex\") then sub(\"=.*\"; \"=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro\") else . end)) | del(.latest_failure) | del(.resume_cursor) '\\'' \"$state_file\" > \"$state_file.tmp\" && mv \"$state_file.tmp\" \"$state_file\"\n  if ! tmux has-session -t \"megaplan-recover-$p\" 2>/dev/null; then\n    tmux new-session -d -s \"megaplan-recover-$p\" \"cd /workspace/astrid && MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 megaplan plan --plan $p --agent hermes --phase-model plan=hermes:deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro --deepseek-provider direct --work-dir /workspace/astrid >> .megaplan/$p-supervisor-recover.log 2>&1 && MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 megaplan auto --plan $p --work-dir /workspace/astrid --phase-timeout 7200 --phase-idle-timeout 1200 --max-review-rework-cycles 3 >> .megaplan/$p-supervisor-recover.log 2>&1\"\n  fi\n}\nwhile true; do\n  {\n    log \"tick\"\n    git status --short --branch || true\n    tmux ls || true\n    p=\"$(current_plan)\"\n    s=\"$(plan_state \"$p\")\"\n    log \"current_plan=$p state=$s\"\n    megaplan chain status --spec \"$SPEC\" || true\n    if tmux has-session -t megaplan-sprint3-direct-recover 2>/dev/null; then\n      log \"manual sprint3 recovery active\"\n    elif [ \"$s\" = \"failed\" ] && quota_failed \"$p\"; then\n      recover_quota_plan \"$p\" || true\n    elif [ \"$s\" = \"done\" ] || [ \"$s\" = \"\" ]; then\n      start_runner\n    elif ! tmux has-session -t megaplan-chain-one-pr 2>/dev/null && ! pgrep -af \"megaplan auto --plan $p\" >/dev/null; then\n      log \"starting auto for active plan $p\"\n      tmux new-session -d -s \"megaplan-auto-$p\" \"cd /workspace/astrid && MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 megaplan auto --plan $p --work-dir /workspace/astrid --phase-timeout 7200 --phase-idle-timeout 1200 --max-review-rework-cycles 3 >> .megaplan/$p-supervisor-auto.log 2>&1\"\n    else\n      log \"active work detected; no action\"\n    fi\n    log \"sleeping\"\n  } >> \"$LOG\" 2>&1\n  sleep 3600\ndone\nEOF\nchmod +x .megaplan/supervise-one-pr-chain.sh\ntmux kill-session -t megaplan-hourly-check 2>/dev/null || true\ntmux kill-session -t megaplan-chain-supervisor 2>/dev/null || true\ntmux new-session -d -s megaplan-chain-supervisor /workspace/astrid/.megaplan/supervise-one-pr-chain.sh\ntmux ls && tail -80 .megaplan/hourly-chain-supervisor.log'",
  "workdir": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid",
  "yield_time_ms": 1000,
  "max_output_tokens": 30000
}

> AGENT

Yes, it’s running again.

Current state:
- Sprint 3 had stalled at `plan`.
- Cause was **Codex quota exhaustion**, not a bad Sprint 3 plan.
- I reset the failed state cursor and reran Sprint 3 planning through **direct DeepSeek API**.
- Sprint 3 planning has now succeeded and produced `plan_v1.md`.
- `megaplan auto` is now running Sprint 3 critique/gate/execution in tmux.
- I replaced the passive hourly logger with an active supervisor: `megaplan-chain-supervisor`.

What was causing the stalls:
1. **Real Astrid plan issue in Sprint 2:** resolver-backed orchestrator execution was incomplete. I fixed that and pushed it to PR #8.
2. **Megaplan provider recovery issue:** Codex quota failures put plans into `failed`, then Megaplan would not rerun the failed phase with another provider without state repair. I worked around it, but Megaplan itself still needs a real fix.
3. **Chain ergonomics issue:** Megaplan does not natively support “one long chain, same PR, push after each sprint, recover and continue.” We’re doing it with wrapper/supervisor scripts.
4. **Codex route is currently unusable:** remaining phases configured as Codex would keep failing, so I switched the active cloud chain’s remaining Codex phases to direct DeepSeek.

So: I fixed the **Sprint 2 implementation problem** in Astrid. I have not yet patched Megaplan itself; I’ve installed an operational recovery path on the cloud runner so this chain can keep moving.

Useful cloud logs:
- Sprint 3 recovery: `/workspace/astrid/.megaplan/sprint3-direct-recover.log`
- Hourly supervisor: `/workspace/astrid/.megaplan/hourly-chain-supervisor.log`
- One-PR chain runner: `/workspace/astrid/.megaplan/cloud-chain-one-pr-push-each.log`

> DEVELOPER

so if i want to launch another tas on that cloud worker, how shoould i do it?

> AGENT

Use a separate tmux session and a separate branch/plan directory, but don’t reuse the current Astrid checkout for another mutating task while this chain is running.

Recommended pattern:

```bash
megaplan cloud exec --cloud-yaml docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/cloud.yaml \
  'cd /workspace && git clone /workspace/astrid astrid-task-foo && cd astrid-task-foo && git checkout -b task/foo'
```

Then start the task in its own tmux session:

```bash
megaplan cloud exec --cloud-yaml docs/megaplan/git-backed-packs/cloud.yaml \
  'tmux new-session -d -s task-foo "cd /workspace/astrid-task-foo && MEGAPLAN_TRUSTED_CONTAINER=1 megaplan auto --plan <plan-name> --work-dir /workspace/astrid-task-foo >> .megaplan/task-foo.log 2>&1"'
```

For a new chain, create/use a separate checkout like `/workspace/astrid-task-foo`, separate branch, separate tmux name, and separate logs. The active `/workspace/astrid` checkout is currently owned by the git-backed-packs chain and PR #8, so launching another mutating task there would risk branch/state collisions.

> DEVELOPER

And how do we set up the early check-in?

> AGENT

Set the cloud supervisor schedule to:

1. **Immediate check** when the task starts.
2. **Early check** after ~10-15 minutes.
3. **Hourly checks** after that.

For a new cloud task, wrap the run in tmux plus a small monitor:

```bash
tmux new-session -d -s task-foo-run \
  'cd /workspace/astrid-task-foo && ./run-task.sh >> .megaplan/task-foo.log 2>&1'

tmux new-session -d -s task-foo-monitor \
  'cd /workspace/astrid-task-foo &&
   ./check-task.sh >> .megaplan/task-foo-monitor.log 2>&1;
   sleep 900;
   ./check-task.sh >> .megaplan/task-foo-monitor.log 2>&1;
   while true; do
     sleep 3600;
     ./check-task.sh >> .megaplan/task-foo-monitor.log 2>&1;
   done'
```

For Megaplan specifically, `check-task.sh` should do roughly:

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -u
cd /workspace/astrid-task-foo

date -Is
tmux ls || true
megaplan status --plan <plan-name> || true
megaplan progress --plan <plan-name> || true
tail -80 .megaplan/<task-log>.log || true
```

For the current Astrid chain, I’d update the existing supervisor to do exactly that: tick now, tick again after 15 minutes, then hourly. The useful policy is “early check catches bad provider/env/branch setup; hourly checks are for long execution drift.”

> DEVELOPER

Will that push it forward automatically if it makes any decisions or block?

> AGENT

Partly, but not enough yet.

The current supervisor can:
- notice a quota-failed plan,
- switch that plan from Codex to direct DeepSeek,
- restart `megaplan auto`,
- restart the one-PR chain runner when a sprint is done.

It does **not** safely make arbitrary product/architecture decisions or resolve normal Megaplan blocks. If Megaplan reaches a real ambiguity, failed tests, merge conflict, or reviewer `needs_rework`, it may keep running only if `megaplan auto` already knows the next step. Otherwise it will log the block and wait until the next intervention.

The right upgrade is an “operator loop” with a narrow decision policy:

- Auto-recover infrastructure issues: quota, dropped SSH, stale tmux, failed provider, dead runner.
- Auto-continue Megaplan states with valid next steps: `planned`, `critiqued`, `gated`, `finalized`, `executed`.
- Auto-accept low-risk defaults that are already documented in the sprint plan.
- Do not auto-decide scope changes, merge conflicts, destructive cleanup, or failing tests that need code judgment.
- Push after each completed sprint.

So: current setup pushes through known infra stalls, not all decision blocks. For the workflow you want, we should add that operator loop explicitly.

> DEVELOPER

And is this documented in the Megaplan skill documentation? If not, can you do that? Is this well engineered or just hacky?

> AGENT

I’ll check the Megaplan skill doc and add the missing cloud-operator pattern if it isn’t already there. I’ll keep the wording honest: this current shell supervisor is an operational shim, while the engineered version belongs as a first-class Megaplan cloud feature.

> AGENT

It was **not** documented beyond the basic cloud lifecycle. I added a `Cloud Operator Loop` section to `/Users/user_c042661f/.codex/skills/megaplan/SKILL.md`.

On engineering quality: the current setup is useful, but it’s a shim.

- **Reasonable operationally:** tmux runner, separate supervisor, early check, hourly checks, provider fallback, push-after-sprint. That is fine for keeping this run moving.
- **Not well engineered as a product abstraction:** it lives as project-local shell around Megaplan state files, and quota recovery required state editing. That should be first-class in Megaplan.
- **Right durable abstraction:** built-in cloud supervision with early/hourly check cadence, explicit provider fallback policy, single-PR chain mode, and push-after-milestone support.

I documented that distinction directly so future runs don’t mistake the shell supervisor for the ideal design.

```json
{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/.codex/skills/megaplan/SKILL.md\n@@\n ## Cloud Mode\n `megaplan cloud` runs a plan inside a provider-managed container with a persistent workspace volume, so the run survives the user's terminal session. Suggest it for long-running plans that would outlast a local session or when the user wants an isolated persistent sandbox. Sprint 1 ships the `railway` provider only; `ssh` and `local` are planned.\n Subcommands: `init`, `build`, `deploy`, `status`, `attach`, `logs`, `exec`, `resume`, `down`, `destroy`.\n Typical flow: `megaplan cloud init` scaffolds `cloud.yaml`; edit it; export the secrets it lists; `megaplan cloud deploy`; then use `status`, `logs`, and `attach` to observe.\n See `docs/cloud.md` for the full reference, including `cloud.yaml` fields, mode behavior (`auto`/`chain`/`idle`), secret handling, and troubleshooting.\n+\n+### Cloud Operator Loop\n+For long-running cloud plans, especially chain runs, do not rely on a passive `tail` or one-off `cloud exec` as the only supervision. Run the plan in one tmux session and a separate monitor/supervisor in another tmux session.\n+\n+Recommended check cadence:\n+1. Check immediately after launch, to catch bad branches, missing secrets, bad provider config, or command syntax.\n+2. Check again after 10-15 minutes, because most cloud setup and first model-call failures surface early.\n+3. Check hourly after that for long execution, review, or chain progress.\n+\n+Use separate cloud workspaces for unrelated mutating tasks. If `/workspace/<repo>` is already running a plan, create a sibling checkout such as `/workspace/<repo>-task-foo`, use a separate branch, separate tmux session names, and separate logs. Do not launch two mutating plans in the same checkout unless the user explicitly wants them to share branch state.\n+\n+An operator loop may automatically handle infrastructure recovery:\n+- restart a dead tmux runner when no active phase process exists;\n+- rerun `megaplan auto` for states with an unambiguous valid next step;\n+- recover provider quota/failure by switching to an already-approved fallback model/provider for the same phase;\n+- continue a chain after a completed milestone;\n+- commit and push after each completed milestone when the user asked for push-after-sprint behavior.\n+\n+An operator loop should not silently decide product or architecture questions, resolve merge conflicts, accept destructive cleanup, or ignore failing tests. Those are implementation decisions, not supervision. Surface them to the user or write a clear ticket unless the plan already contains an explicit settled decision that covers the case.\n+\n+Today this operator loop is usually a small project-local shell script under `.megaplan/` plus tmux. Treat that as an operational shim, not the ideal abstraction. The durable Megaplan feature should be first-class cloud supervision: built-in early check, hourly tick, provider fallback policy, single-PR chain mode, and push-after-milestone support.\n ## Tickets\n `megaplan ticket new` creates a repo-scoped issue ticket. Use it when:\n*** End Patch\n"
}

{
  "cmd": "cat <<'EOF' | megaplan ticket new \"Make cloud chain supervision first-class\" --tags cloud,chain,supervision,reliability,provider-fallback -\nDuring the Astrid git-backed-packs chain, the cloud run needed a project-local shell supervisor to keep work moving. That worked operationally, but it is the wrong abstraction for Megaplan long term.\n\n## What happened\n\n- The user wanted a long Megaplan chain to run on cloud, all remaining milestones on one PR, with a push after each sprint.\n- The built-in chain flow is milestone/PR oriented, so we wrapped `megaplan chain start --one --no-push --no-git-refresh` in a shell loop that commits/pushes to PR #8 after each completed sprint.\n- Sprint 2 exposed a real Astrid issue and was fixed, but review then hit Codex quota. Resume retried the same Codex route and stayed stuck.\n- Sprint 3 failed before planning because `plan=codex:medium` hit quota. Recovering required manually editing `state.json` back from `failed` to `initialized`, deleting failure cursors, changing phase routing to direct DeepSeek, and rerunning the phase.\n- We added a cloud tmux supervisor that logs immediately/hourly, detects quota-failed states, rewrites phase routing to an approved fallback, restarts `megaplan auto`, and restarts the chain runner when a sprint completes.\n\n## Why this is a Megaplan product gap\n\nThe shell supervisor is useful but hacky:\n\n- It edits private state files directly.\n- It encodes provider fallback outside Megaplan.\n- It cannot distinguish every valid recovery from real product/architecture decisions.\n- It relies on tmux/session naming conventions.\n- It implements single-PR chain behavior outside the chain engine.\n- It gives the user operational confidence, but not a durable or reusable cloud abstraction.\n\n## Desired first-class behavior\n\nBuild a native cloud operator/supervisor mode with explicit policy:\n\n1. Early check-in cadence\n   - run an immediate post-launch check;\n   - run an early check after 10-15 minutes;\n   - run hourly checks after that;\n   - write structured status snapshots, not just raw logs.\n\n2. Safe auto-advance\n   - continue states with unambiguous valid next steps;\n   - restart a dead runner when no active phase process exists;\n   - resume `megaplan auto` for active plans that are not blocked;\n   - continue a chain after a completed milestone.\n\n3. Provider fallback and quota recovery\n   - detect provider quota/rate-limit/auth failures as infrastructure failures;\n   - allow retrying the failed phase with an approved fallback provider without hand-editing state;\n   - preserve an audit trail showing original provider, fallback provider, reason, and affected phase;\n   - never silently change model class for product decisions unless the chain/profile explicitly allows it.\n\n4. Single-PR chain mode\n   - support `chain start` options for one long branch/PR across multiple milestones;\n   - commit and push after each completed milestone;\n   - update the same PR body/status summary;\n   - avoid automatic base-branch refresh that stomps an active long-running branch unless requested.\n\n5. Decision boundary\n   - auto-handle infrastructure: dropped SSH/WebSocket, stale tmux/process, provider quota, dead runner, known retryable phase failures;\n   - do not auto-resolve merge conflicts, failing tests that require code judgment, destructive cleanup, or scope/architecture ambiguities;\n   - surface those as blocked states with a concise reason and suggested next action.\n\n6. Multiple tasks on one cloud worker\n   - document or implement a helper for sibling checkouts (`/workspace/repo-task-name`), separate branches, separate tmux/session names, and separate logs;\n   - prevent accidental launch of two mutating plans in the same checkout unless explicitly forced.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- A cloud chain can be launched in supervised mode and gets immediate, early, and hourly status snapshots.\n- A quota failure in a phase can be retried with an approved fallback provider through a supported command/API, without state-file surgery.\n- A chain can run multiple milestones on one PR and push after each milestone.\n- The supervisor records what it did and why in machine-readable artifacts.\n- The supervisor refuses to make product/architecture decisions outside declared policy.\n- Documentation explains the recommended operator loop and when to use isolated sibling checkouts.\n\n## Related observation\n\nI added a temporary `Cloud Operator Loop` section to the local Megaplan skill docs so agents know the current operational pattern. That should become product documentation once the native feature exists.\nEOF",

  "yield_time_ms": 1000,
  "max_output_tokens": 12000
}

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/data/instructions.md\n@@\n ## Cloud Mode\n `megaplan cloud` runs a plan inside a provider-managed container with a persistent workspace volume, so the run survives the user's terminal session. Suggest it for long-running plans that would outlast a local session or when the user wants an isolated persistent sandbox. Sprint 1 ships the `railway` provider only; `ssh` and `local` are planned.\n Subcommands: `init`, `build`, `deploy`, `status`, `attach`, `logs`, `exec`, `resume`, `down`, `destroy`.\n Typical flow: `megaplan cloud init` scaffolds `cloud.yaml`; edit it; export the secrets it lists; `megaplan cloud deploy`; then use `status`, `logs`, and `attach` to observe.\n See `docs/cloud.md` for the full reference, including `cloud.yaml` fields, mode behavior (`auto`/`chain`/`idle`), secret handling, and troubleshooting.\n+\n+### Cloud Operator Loop\n+For long-running cloud plans, especially chain runs, do not rely on a passive `tail` or one-off `cloud exec` as the only supervision. Run the plan in one tmux session and a separate monitor/supervisor in another tmux session.\n+\n+Recommended check cadence:\n+1. Check immediately after launch, to catch bad branches, missing secrets, bad provider config, or command syntax.\n+2. Check again after 10-15 minutes, because most cloud setup and first model-call failures surface early.\n+3. Check hourly after that for long execution, review, or chain progress.\n+\n+Use separate cloud workspaces for unrelated mutating tasks. If `/workspace/<repo>` is already running a plan, create a sibling checkout such as `/workspace/<repo>-task-foo`, use a separate branch, separate tmux session names, and separate logs. Do not launch two mutating plans in the same checkout unless the user explicitly wants them to share branch state.\n+\n+An operator loop may automatically handle infrastructure recovery:\n+- restart a dead tmux runner when no active phase process exists;\n+- rerun `megaplan auto` for states with an unambiguous valid next step;\n+- recover provider quota/failure by switching to an already-approved fallback model/provider for the same phase;\n+- continue a chain after a completed milestone;\n+- commit and push after each completed milestone when the user asked for push-after-sprint behavior.\n+\n+An operator loop should not silently decide product or architecture questions, resolve merge conflicts, accept destructive cleanup, or ignore failing tests. Those are implementation decisions, not supervision. Surface them to the user or write a clear ticket unless the plan already contains an explicit settled decision that covers the case.\n+\n+Today this operator loop is usually a small project-local shell script under `.megaplan/` plus tmux. Treat that as an operational shim, not the ideal abstraction. The durable Megaplan feature should be first-class cloud supervision: built-in early check, hourly tick, provider fallback policy, single-PR chain mode, and push-after-milestone support.\n ## Tickets\n `megaplan ticket new` creates a repo-scoped issue ticket. Use it when:\n*** End Patch\n"
}
